    <li> Location of first aid facilities </li>
    <li> Emergency procedures</li>
</ul>
<p>Generic instruction and orientation could be provided at a corporate or district level. In some cases workers could carry documentation as proof that they have received generic orientation for their respective occupation or trade. Generic orientation and training that includes an expiry date will help ensure that workers receive up-to-date information.</p>
<p><strong>Where to get further information</strong><br />
Some examples of the various sources of information on orientation and training are
</p>
<ul>
    <li> The Canadian Centre for Occupational Health and Safety (CCOHS) maintains a web site on which they provide information on various topics, including a number of those listed in section 3.23. Examples include: rights and responsibilities, working alone, WHMIS, and violence in the workplace. CCOHS also maintains a Youth portal on the site, with topics specific to young workers. The home page for CCOHS is found at <a href="http://www.ccohs.ca/" target="_blank">http://www.ccohs.ca/</a>.</li>
    <li> WorkSafeBC maintains a <a href="/en/health-safety/education-training-certification/young-new-worker">Young Worker portal</a> on its web site. The portal provides a range of materials and helpful links, including a checklist for training and orientation, a program on rights and responsibilities, and information on typical accidents young workers have experienced.</li>
    <li> Any health and safety association in an industry may also have information available. </li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Children in the workplace</strong><br />
In British Columbia, the <cite>Employment Standards Act ("ESA")</cite> sets out age requirements for the employment of children. Specifically, the <cite>ESA</cite> requires that an employer may not hire a child under the age of 15 without the written consent of the child's parent or legal guardian. An employer may not employ a child under the age of 12 without the permission of the Director of Employment Standards, which is granted through a permit. The <cite>Employment Standards Regulation</cite> further sets out working conditions for children, including the requirement that a child may only work under the direct supervision of a person who is 19 years of age or older. The <cite>Employment Standards Regulation</cite> sets out separate conditions for children working in the entertainment industry, and excludes certain types of work, such as babysitting, from the above requirements.</p>
<p>A WorkSafeBC prevention officer, or any other person, who encounters a potential violation of the <cite>ESA</cite> or the <cite>Employment Standards Regulation</cite> should refer the matter to the Employment Standards Branch. Contact information for the regional Branch locations can be found at: <a href="http://www.labour.gov.bc.ca/esb/contact/welcome.htm" target="_blank">http://www.labour.gov.bc.ca/esb/contact/welcome.htm</a></p>
<p>Individuals seeking additional information may want to view the following fact sheets. These fact sheets have been developed by the Employment Standards Branch to outline conditions for the employment of children, the <a href="http://www.labour.gov.bc.ca/esb/young/guide.pdf" target="_blank">first aimed at parents</a> and the <a href="http://www.labour.gov.bc.ca/esb/facshts/pdfs/youth_general.pdf" target="_blank">second aimed at employers and the general public</a>.</p>
<a name="EndSectionNumber:G3.23" style="padding: 0px;"></a>
                <h4>Guidelines - Part 3 - Joint Health and Safety Committees</h4>
<ul class = "navHorizPipe navRegTools"><li><a href="/law-policy/occupational-health-safety/searchable-ohs-regulation/ohs-regulation/part-03-rights-and-responsibilities#1DDB8ADE5B194B7A8C3C45098B3C5E7F"/>Related Regulations</a></li><li><a class='add_remove add_to_handbook' name='F35AE5EB114E4345A261C9F1130ABEE6' id='F35AE5EB114E4345A261C9F1130ABEE6' href='#'><span class='handbook_title'>+ Add to My Handbook</a></span></li></ul><a name="SectionNumber:G3.26" style="padding: 0px;"></a>
<h6>G3.26 Evaluation of joint committees</h6>
<p>Issued consequential to April 3, 2017 Regulatory Amendment; Editorial Revision April 6, 2020</p>
<p><strong>Regulatory excerpt</strong><br />
Sections 3.26(2) and (3) of the <cite>OHS Regulation</cite> ("<cite>Regulation</cite>") state:</p>
<blockquote>
<p class="subL1">(2) An employer must ensure that, with respect to each of the employer's joint committees, a written evaluation is conducted annually by</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) the co-chairs of the joint committee or, with respect to each co-chair, the member or members of the joint committee designated by the co-chair, or</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) the employer or a person retained by the employer.</p>
<p class="subL1">(3) The evaluation must contain, but is not limited to, the following information:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) whether or not, throughout the period of time that is the subject of the evaluation,</p>
<p class="subL3">(i) the joint committee met the membership requirements under section 33(a) to&nbsp;(d) of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(ii) worker representatives on the joint committee were selected in accordance with section&nbsp;34 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(iii) employer representatives on the joint committee were selected in accordance with section&nbsp;35 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(iv) the joint committee fulfilled each of its duties and functions under section&nbsp;36 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(v) the joint committee met regularly as required under section&nbsp;37(2) of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(vi) the employer met the requirements under section&nbsp;39 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite> in respect of the written recommendations sent to the employer by the joint committee with a written request for a response from the employer, if any,</p>
<p class="subL3">(vii) each member of the joint committee received the time off from work the member was entitled to receive under section&nbsp;40 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(viii) each member of the joint committee attended the occupational health and safety training courses the member was entitled to attend under section&nbsp;41 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(ix) the employer provided to the joint committee the equipment, premises, clerical personnel and information the employer was required to provide under section&nbsp;42 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(x) the joint committee prepared reports of its meetings and provided copies to the employer as required under section&nbsp;43(1) of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>,</p>
<p class="subL3">(xi) the employer met the requirements of posting and keeping posted committee information as set out in section&nbsp;44 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>, and</p>
<p class="subL3">(xii) each member of the joint committee received the instruction and training the employer was required to ensure was provided to the member under section 3.27 of this regulation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) an assessment of the effectiveness of the joint committee's rules of procedure as established under section&nbsp;37(1) of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>;</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) an assessment of the overall effectiveness of the joint committee.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>Section 36 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite> ("<cite>Act</cite>") states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>A joint committee has the following duties and functions in relation to its workplace:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) to identify situations that may be unhealthy or unsafe for workers and advise on effective systems for responding to those situations;</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) to consider and expeditiously deal with complaints relating to the health and safety of workers;</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) to consult with workers and the employer on issues related to occupational health and safety and occupational environment;</p>
<p class="subL2">(d) to make recommendations to the employer and the workers for the improvement of the occupational health and safety and occupational environment of workers;</p>
<p class="subL2">(e) to make recommendations to the employer on educational programs promoting the health and safety of workers and compliance with the OHS provisions and the regulations and to monitor their effectiveness;</p>
<p class="subL2">(f) to advise the employer on programs and policies required under the regulations for the workplace and to monitor their effectiveness;</p>
<p class="subL2">(g) to advise the employer on proposed changes to the workplace, including significant proposed changes to equipment and machinery, or the work processes that may affect the health or safety of workers;</p>
<p class="subL2">(h) to ensure that accident investigations and regular inspections are carried out as required by the OHS provisions and the regulations;</p>
<p class="subL2">(i) to participate in inspections, investigations and inquiries as provided in the OHS provisions and the regulations;</p>
<p class="subL2">(j) to carry out any other duties and functions prescribed by regulation.</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Purpose of guideline</strong><br />
The purpose of this  guideline is to provide direction in choosing an evaluation tool to meet the requirement for a written evaluation of the effectiveness of a joint committee.</p>
<p><strong>Background</strong><br />
The joint committee is a key element of the internal responsibility system. Its purpose is to act as a forum for the employer and workers to work together to improve health and safety in the workplace. A well functioning committee permits health and safety issues to be brought forward and acted upon quickly and effectively, and will contribute significantly to health and safety in the workplace.</p>
<p>Section 3.26 requires a written evaluation to be conducted annually to determine the effectiveness of the joint committee. The intent of the evaluation is to determine whether the joint committee is generally in compliance with the <cite>Act</cite> and <cite>Regulation</cite>, and to assess whether the joint committee has been effective in fulfilling its role so that improvements can be considered and implemented.</p>
<p><strong>Written evaluation</strong><br />
The obligation is on the employer to ensure a written evaluation is carried out. In accordance with section 3.26(2), the evaluation itself may be undertaken by the co-chairs of the committee or by a person retained by the employer.</p>
<p>The minimum topics that the evaluation must include are set out in section 3.26(3) of the <cite>Regulation</cite>. Section 3.26 requires the evaluation to review a number of procedural elements, such as whether membership requirements were met, reports were prepared and posted, etc.  Section 3.26 also requires that the evaluation include an assessment of the overall effectiveness of the joint committee. The purpose of the evaluation is to allow a joint committee, after a year of operation, to examine its effectiveness and implement improvements in following years.</p>
<p>In looking at overall effectiveness, those undertaking the review should be particularly mindful of the duties and functions of the joint committee contained in section&nbsp;36 of the <cite>Act</cite>, as required by section 3.26(3)(a)(iv) of the <cite>Regulation</cite>. The evaluation should consider how well the committee has functioned to fulfill each of those duties and functions and should identify specific areas where improvements can be made and how those improvements can be implemented.</p>
<p><strong>Evaluation tool</strong><br />
In order to assist those persons conducting the evaluation, WorkSafeBC has developed an evaluation tool that includes all of the required elements of the evaluation. It can be found online at:<br />
<a href="/en/health-safety/create-manage/joint-health-safety-committees" target="_blank">https://www.worksafebc.com/en/health-safety/create-manage/joint-health-safety-committees</a>.</p>
<p>Use of this specific tool is not mandatory; other tools can be used as long as they meet or exceed all the requirements for an evaluation as specified in section 3.26(3) of the <cite>Regulation</cite>. Employers may want to review Certificate of Recognition (COR) audit tools for joint committees, or create a customized tool for their committee capturing all the required elements.</p>
<a name="EndSectionNumber:G3.26" style="padding: 0px;"></a>

<a name="SectionNumber:G3.27" style="padding: 0px;"></a>
<h6>G3.27 Minimum training requirements for new joint committee members or worker health and safety representatives</h6>
<p>Issued consequential to April 3, 2017 Regulatory Amendment; Editorial Revision April 6, 2020</p>
<p><strong>Regulatory excerpt</strong><br />
Sections 3.27(2) to (5) of the <cite>OHS Regulation</cite> ("<cite>Regulation</cite>") state:</p>
<blockquote>
<p class="subL1">(2) The employer must ensure that each member of the employer's joint
committees who was selected on or after April 3, 2017 to be a member receives, as soon as practicable but no more than 6 months after becoming a member, a total of at least 8 hours of instruction and training, as set out in subsection (4).</p>
<p class="subL1">(3) The employer must ensure that the worker health and safety representative at each of the employer's workplaces who was selected on or after April 3, 2017 to be a representative receives, as soon as practicable but no more than 6 months after becoming a representative, a total of at least 4 hours of instruction and training, as set out in subsection (5).</p>
<p class="subL1">(4) The instruction and training referred to in subsection (2) must include the following topics:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) the duties and functions of a joint committee under section&nbsp;36 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>;</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) the rules of procedure of the joint committee as established under or set out in section&nbsp;37 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>;</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) the requirements respecting investigations under sections 69 to&nbsp;72 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>;</p>
<p class="subL2">(d) the requirements respecting inspections under sections 3.5, 3.7 and 3.8 of this regulation and how to make regular inspections under section 3.5 of this regulation</p>
<p class="subL2">(e) the requirements respecting refusal of unsafe work under section 3.12 of this regulation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(f) the requirements respecting the evaluation of joint committees under section 3.26 of this regulation.</p>
<p class="subL1">(5) The instruction and training referred to in subsection (3) must include the topics described in subsection (4)(a), (c), (d) and (e).</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Purpose of guideline</strong><br />
The purpose of this guideline is to clarify the time frame requirements for training of new joint committee members and to outline training options to meet the minimum training requirements.</p>
<p><strong>Time frame for training</strong><br />
Training for new joint committee members and worker health and safety representatives must be provided as soon as practicable, but no more than six months after becoming a member. Section 1.1 of the <cite>Regulation</cite> defines "practicable" as something that is "reasonably capable of being done." Therefore the training must be done as soon as it is reasonably capable of being done. Six months is an outer time limit that should only be reached in limited circumstances.</p>
<p><strong>Training options</strong><br />
<strong>Worker health and safety representatives</strong><br />
Worker health and safety representatives are required in every workplace with more than nine but fewer than 20 workers regularly employed.</p>
<p>New representatives require a minimum of four hours of training that encompasses the topics outlined in sections 3.27(4)(a), (c), (d), and (e) of the <cite>Regulation</cite>. WorkSafeBC has developed a four-hour online training program that is available to all workers at:<br />
<a href="/en/health-safety/education-training-certification/joint-health-safety-committee-member" target="_blank">https://www.worksafebc.com/en/health-safety/education-training-certification/joint-health-safety-committee-member</a>.</p>
<p>This training may be completed at the worker's convenience and pace; a record of successful completion will be available for printing and/or emailing.</p>
<p>Employers may choose other formats for training including using external providers who offer training that meets the requirements listed above or internal training designed to meet the requirements.</p>
<p>The instruction and training provided to worker representatives should include the following learning outcomes:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) Explain the purposes of the <cite>Act</cite>, <cite>Regulation</cite>, and OHS Guidelines</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) Summarize the duties and functions of a worker representative</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) Explain the role of the worker representative in accident investigations, regular inspections, and the work refusal process</p>
<p class="subL2">(d) Summarize educational requirements for worker representatives (section&nbsp;41 of the <cite>Act</cite> and section 3.27 of the <cite>Regulation</cite>)</p>
<p class="subL2">(e) Explain worker protection in relation to prohibited action (section&nbsp;47 of the <cite>Act</cite>)</p>
<p class="subL2">(f) Identify when an employer is required to consult with the worker representative</p>
<p><strong>Joint committee members</strong><br />
Joint committee members are required in every workplace with 20 or more workers regularly employed.</p>
<p>New committee members require a minimum of eight hours of training that encompasses all of the topics outlined in section 3.27(4) of the <cite>Regulation</cite>. WorkSafeBC has developed a course that can be delivered in a variety of formats, including blended learning (independent e-learning and in-class facilitated), traditional classroom, or facilitator-led webinar.
</p>
<p>The materials for this course (facilitator guide, student guide, and tools) are available for downloading at:<br />
<a href="/en/health-safety/education-training-certification/joint-health-safety-committee-member" target="_blank">https://www.worksafebc.com/en/health-safety/education-training-certification/joint-health-safety-committee-member</a>.</p>
<p>Employers may choose other formats for training including using external providers who offer training that meets the requirements listed above or internal training designed to meet the requirements.</p>
<p>The requirements allow flexibility regarding delivery methods and do not require the training to occur in one session.</p>
<p>The instruction and training provided to the Joint Health and Safety Committee members should include the following learning outcomes:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) Explain the purposes of the <cite>Act</cite>, <cite>Regulation</cite>, and OHS Guidelines</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) Summarize the duties and functions of the joint committee</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) Explain the role of the joint committee in accident investigations, regular inspections, and the work refusal process</p>
<p class="subL2">(d) Summarize educational requirements for joint committee members (section&nbsp;41 of the <cite>Act</cite> and section 3.27 of the <cite>Regulation</cite>)</p>
<p class="subL2">(e) Conduct an individual needs assessment for the purposes of selecting training in accordance with section&nbsp;41</p>
<p class="subL2">(f) Explain worker protection in relation to prohibited action (section&nbsp;47 of the <cite>Act</cite>)</p>
<p class="subL2">(g) Identify when an employer is required to consult with the joint committee</p>
<p class="subL2">(h) Differentiate between the roles of the committee co-chairs and joint committee members</p>
<p class="subL2">(i) Explain the purpose of a joint committee's Terms of Reference (or Rules of Procedure)</p>
<p class="subL2">(j) Apply the policies and procedures outlined in the joint committee's Terms of Reference</p>
<p class="subL2">(k) List the traits of an effective joint committee and explain the purpose of conducting an evaluation of the joint committee's effectiveness</p>
<p><strong>Mandatory training</strong><br />
Section 3.27 requires employers to ensure training is provided for new members of a joint committee, or new worker health and safety representatives, selected on or after April 3, 2017.</p>
<p>Under employment standards legislation (administered by the Employment Standards Branch of B.C.), employers are required to pay for the training employees need in order to learn how to do their job at the employer's business, and the time spent in that training is considered work. Training on how to perform the role of a joint committee member or worker health and safety representative is considered training employees need to perform their job.</p>
<p><strong>Educational leave entitlement</strong><br />
Under section&nbsp;41 of the <cite>Act</cite>, each member of a joint committee and each worker health and safety representative is entitled to eight hours of annual educational leave for the purposes of attending occupational health and safety training courses. This educational leave is an entitlement, and in accordance with section 3.27(10), is in addition to the mandatory minimum training for new members of a joint committee and new worker health and safety representatives.</p>
<a name="EndSectionNumber:G3.27" style="padding: 0px;"></a>
                <h4>Guidelines - Part 3 - Participation in Investigations</h4>
<ul class = "navHorizPipe navRegTools"><li><a href="/law-policy/occupational-health-safety/searchable-ohs-regulation/ohs-regulation/part-03-rights-and-responsibilities#F72DFB3E79D94D4485FDFA887D41CA50"/>Related Regulations</a></li><li><a class='add_remove add_to_handbook' name='F809842C908A4E768968E950DC7589B6' id='F809842C908A4E768968E950DC7589B6' href='#'><span class='handbook_title'>+ Add to My Handbook</a></span></li></ul><a name="SectionNumber:G3.28" style="padding: 0px;"></a>
<h6>G3.28 Participation in employer incident investigations</h6>
<p>Issued consequential to April 3, 2017 Regulatory Amendment; Editorial Revision April 6, 2020</p>
<p><strong>Regulatory excerpt</strong><br />
Section 3.28 of the <cite>OHS Regulation</cite> ("<cite>Regulation</cite>") states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>For the purposes of section 70(2) of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite>, the following activities are prescribed:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) assisting the persons carrying out the investigation with gathering information relating to the investigation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) assisting the persons carrying out the investigation with analyzing the information gathered during the investigation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) assisting the persons carrying out the investigation with identifying any corrective actions necessary to prevent recurrence of similar incidents.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>Section 70 of the <cite>Workers Compensation Act</cite> ("<cite>Act</cite>") states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p class="subL1">(1) An investigation required under this Division must be carried out by persons knowledgeable about the type of work involved and, if they are reasonably available, with the participation of the employer or a representative of the employer and a worker representative.</p>
<p class="subL1">(2) For the purposes of subsection (1), the participation of the employer or a representative of the employer and a worker representative includes, but is not limited to, the following activities:</p>
<p class="subL2">(a) viewing the scene of the incident with the persons carrying out the investigation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(b) providing advice to the persons carrying out the investigation respecting the methods used to carry out the investigation, the scope of the investigation, or any other aspect of the investigation;</p>
<p class="subL2">(c) other activities, as prescribed by the Board.</p>
<p class="subL1">(3) The employer must make every reasonable effort to have available for interview by a person conducting the investigation, or by an officer, all witnesses to the incident and any other persons whose presence might be necessary for a proper investigation of the incident.</p>
<p class="subL1">(4) The employer must record the names, addresses and telephone numbers of persons referred to in subsection (3).</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Purpose of guideline</strong><br />
The purpose of this  guideline is to clarify the requirement for participation in employer incident investigations.</p>
<p><strong>Participation in employer incident investigations</strong><br />
Section 69 of the <cite>Act</cite> specifies which incidents must be investigated by an employer, and requires that both a preliminary investigation (section&nbsp;71) and a full investigation (section&nbsp;72) be conducted. Section&nbsp;70 of the <cite>Act</cite> specifies that these investigations must be carried out by persons knowledgeable about the type of work involved. It also requires the participation of the employer or employer representative, and a worker representative, if they are reasonably available.</p>
<p>Pursuant to section&nbsp;70(2) of the <cite>Act</cite> and section 3.28 of the <cite>Regulation</cite>, the participation of a worker representative includes, but is not limited to, the following:
</p>
<ul>
    <li>Viewing the scene of the incident with the persons carrying out the investigation</li>
    <li>Providing advice respecting the methods used to carry out the investigation, the scope of the investigation, or any other aspect of the investigation</li>
    <li>Assisting the persons carrying out the investigation with:</li>
    <ul>
        <li>Gathering information relating to the investigation</li>
        <li>Analyzing the information gathered during the investigation</li>
        <li>Identifying any corrective actions necessary to prevent recurrence of similar incidents</li>
    </ul>
</ul>
<p><strong>Ensuring participation</strong><br />
Employers are expected to ensure the participation of worker representatives in incident investigations as this plays an important part in maintaining healthy and safe workplaces. For more information on how this may be achieved, and concerns about inadequate participation, please refer to OHS Guideline <a href="/en/law-policy/occupational-health-safety/searchable-ohs-regulation/ohs-guidelines/guidelines-for-workers-compensation-act#SectionNumber:G-P2-70-1">G-P2-70-1 Participation by worker representatives in incident investigations</a>.</p>
<a name="EndSectionNumber:G3.28" style="padding: 0px;"></a>

However, WorkSafeBC does not warrant the accuracy or the completeness of the online Act or Regulation, and neither WorkSafeBC nor its board of directors, employees or agents shall be liable to any person for any loss or damage of any nature, whether arising out of negligence or otherwise, arising from the use of the online versions. Employers are legally obligated to make a copy of the Workers Compensation Act and the Occupational Health and Safety Regulation readily available for review by workers. The circumstances under which WorkSafeBC may consider an employer&#39;s providing access to electronic versions of the Act and Regulation to have satisfied this obligation are described in OHS guideline G-P2-21(2)(f).
            </div>
        </div>

<script>
    $(document).ready(function () {
        actionUrl = $('#emailForm').attr("action");
        idOfParent = 'F5EC284DA4DF4F6597ED505681721ADE';

        if ($('ul.navHorizPipe.navRegTools li a.add_remove').not('.remove_from_handbook').length == 0) {
            //alert("there are no items with 'add' next to them");
            $(".add_remove[name='F5EC284DA4DF4F6597ED505681721ADE'] .handbook_title").html('- Remove From My Handbook');
            $(".add_remove[name='F5EC284DA4DF4F6597ED505681721ADE']").removeClass('add_to_handbook').addClass('remove_from_handbook');
        }
    });

    //add loading overlay and remove
    var interval = null;
    $('.handbook_toolbar .options .option a.download').click(function (event) {
        event.preventDefault();
        var loadingOverlay = $('<div class="loading_overlay modal-backdrop"><div class="loading_objects"><span>Preparing Download...</span><i class="fa fa-spinner fa-pulse"></i></div></div>');
        loadingOverlay.appendTo(document.body).hide().fadeIn();

        var keyValue = Math.floor(1000 + Math.random() * 9000);
        var downloadUrl = "/ohsregulation/download-handbook-section?downloadkey=" + keyValue + "&parentId=F5EC284DA4DF4F6597ED505681721ADE";
        window.location.href = downloadUrl;

        var interval = setInterval(function () {
            var cookieValue = getCookieValue(keyValue);
            if (cookieValue != null && cookieValue != undefined) {
                clearInterval(interval);
                loadingOverlay.fadeOut(3000).hide().remove();
            }
        }, 3000);
    });
    function getCookieValue(name) {
        cookieList = document.cookie.split('; ');
        cookies = {};
        for (i = cookieList.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
            cookie = cookieList[i].split('=');
            cookies[cookie[0]] = cookie[1];
        }
        return cookies[name];
    };
    
    $("#emailForm").submit(function () {
        input = $('#emailForm').find('input[name="email"]').val();
        $(".email_sending").show();
        $("input.email_submit").hide();
        $("button.email_close").css("right", "23px");
        $.post(actionUrl, { email: input, parentId: idOfParent })
            .always(function (data) {
                if (data.success === true) {
                    $(".email_sending span").html('Your email has been sent successfully!');
                    $(".email_sending i").removeClass('fa-spinner fa-pulse').addClass('fa-check');
                    $("button.email_close").text('Close');
                    //alert("Your email has been sent");

                } else {
                    $(".email_sending span").html('There was an error sending your email');
                    $(".email_sending i").removeClass('fa-spinner fa-pulse').addClass('fa-exclamation-circle');
                    $("button.email_close").text('Close');
                    //alert("There was an error sending your email");
                }
            });
        return false;
    });
</script>

    <script>
        (function () {
            // Scope to THIS nav only (safe if multiple language navs exist)
            var root = document.currentScript.closest('nav[aria-label="Language selector"]');
            if (!root) return;

            var anchors = root.querySelectorAll('ul li a');
            anchors.forEach(function (a) {
                var href = (a.getAttribute('href') || '').trim();
                var text = (a.textContent || '').replace(/\u00A0/g, ' ').trim();

                var emptyHref = !href || href === '#' || /^javascript:/i.test(href);
                var emptyText = text.length === 0;

                if (emptyHref || emptyText) {
                    var li = a.closest('li');
                    (li || a).remove();
                }
            });

            // If everything got removed, hide the whole section
            var list = root.querySelector('ul');
            if (list && list.children.length === 0) {
                root.remove();
            }
        })();
    </script>
</div>

<script>
(function () {
  // Scope to THIS nav only (safe if multiple language navs exist)
  var root = document.currentScript.closest('nav[aria-label="Language selector"]');
  if (!root) return;

  var anchors = root.querySelectorAll('ul li a');
  anchors.forEach(function (a) {
    var href = (a.getAttribute('href') || '').trim();
    var text = (a.textContent || '').replace(/\u00A0/g, ' ').trim();

    var emptyHref = !href || href === '#' || /^javascript:/i.test(href);
    var emptyText = text.length === 0;

    if (emptyHref || emptyText) {
      var li = a.closest('li');
      (li || a).remove();
    }
  });

  // If everything got removed, hide the whole section
  var list = root.querySelector('ul');
  if (list && list.children.length === 0) {
    root.remove();
  }
})();
</script>
